The referee did NOT find a wrong formula. He found that our priority narrative is
substantively false and that two load-bearing proofs are not verifiable. Specifically:

(1) PRIORITY. We omitted the Erdos-measure / golden-ratio Bernoulli convolution / L^q
    spectrum literature, which is not "nearby work" but the existing theory of the very
    same finite counting object:
      - Sidorov-Vershik: representation frequencies f_m(k) from the first m Fibonacci
        weights are the vertex frequencies of the Fibonacci graph, and 2^{-m} f_m(k)
        gives finite approximations to the Erdos measure. Their f_m(k) differs from our
        finite Fibonacci subset-sum coefficients only by an indexing shift.
      - Hu (TAMS): local dimensions of the golden-ratio Bernoulli convolution — the
        direct early source our "normalised log multiplicity" must be compared against.
      - Lau-Ngai (1998): explicit L^q spectrum of the golden-ratio Bernoulli convolution
        for all q > 0.
      - Feng: extended the explicit golden-case L^q spectrum from q > 0 to all real q and
        identified a non-differentiable phase transition at negative q; also Feng-Olivier.
    Consequence: our novelty claims for "one-layer / finite-window all-real pressure" and
    for the "first-order freezing transition" DO NOT STAND. The positive-q pressure
    formula for the golden-ratio Bernoulli convolution predates this manuscript by about
    thirty years.

    Required: cite all of the above accurately, and REDEFINE the main theorems so that
    what is claimed as ours is only what actually remains ours after that literature is
    credited. Say plainly in the introduction what is recovered from known results and
    what is new. If after this a theorem has no surviving novelty, demote it to a
    recorded consistency check with the prior result named, or delete it. A smaller
    honest paper is the goal; do not manufacture replacement claims.

(2) PROOFS. Two steps are not at a verifiable standard and must be repaired or removed:
      - Lemma 5.7, the normalisation transfer to Stern-Brocot / Knauf pressure. This
        carries Theorem 5.8 and most thermodynamic conclusions downstream.
      - Theorem 5.12, the parameter-uniform multivariate smooth-point asymptotics and the
        unbounded-cost truncation. This carries the finite prime support part.
    Either give a complete proof at referee standard, or weaken the statement to what you
    can actually prove and propagate the weakening everywhere it is used. Do not leave a
    gap covered by wording.

(3) VENUE. The target is no longer TAMS. Retarget to Journal of Number Theory: compress
    the transfer-theoretic material substantially and restate the main theorems for that
    audience. Update submission_metadata.md and the cover letter accordingly.

## Instruction Priority

Superpowers skills override default system prompt behavior, but **user instructions always take precedence**:

1. **User's explicit instructions** (CLAUDE.md, GEMINI.md, AGENTS.md, direct requests) â€” highest priority
2. **Superpowers skills** â€” override default system behavior where they conflict
3. **Default system prompt** â€” lowest priority

If CLAUDE.md, GEMINI.md, or AGENTS.md says "don't use TDD" and a skill says "always use TDD," follow the user's instructions. The user is in control.

## How to Access Skills

**In Claude Code:** Use the `Skill` tool. When you invoke a skill, its content is loaded and presented to youâ€”follow it directly. Never use the Read tool on skill files.

**In Copilot CLI:** Use the `skill` tool. Skills are auto-discovered from installed plugins. The `skill` tool works the same as Claude Code's `Skill` tool.

**In Gemini CLI:** Skills activate via the `activate_skill` tool. Gemini loads skill metadata at session start and activates the full content on demand.

**In other environments:** Check your platform's documentation for how skills are loaded.

## Platform Adaptation

Skills use Claude Code tool names. Non-CC platforms: see `references/copilot-tools.md` (Copilot CLI), `references/codex-tools.md` (Codex) for tool equivalents. Gemini CLI users get the tool mapping loaded automatically via GEMINI.md.

# Using Skills

## The Rule

**Invoke relevant or requested skills BEFORE any response or action.** Even a 1% chance a skill might apply means that you should invoke the skill to check. If an invoked skill turns out to be wrong for the situation, you don't need to use it.

```dot
digraph skill_flow {
    "User message received" [shape=doublecircle];
    "About to EnterPlanMode?" [shape=doublecircle];
    "Already brainstormed?" [shape=diamond];
    "Invoke brainstorming skill" [shape=box];
    "Might any skill apply?" [shape=diamond];
    "Invoke Skill tool" [shape=box];
    "Announce: 'Using [skill] to [purpose]'" [shape=box];
    "Has checklist?" [shape=diamond];
    "Create TodoWrite todo per item" [shape=box];
    "Follow skill exactly" [shape=box];
    "Respond (including clarifications)" [shape=doublecircle];

    "About to EnterPlanMode?" -> "Already brainstormed?";
    "Already brainstormed?" -> "Invoke brainstorming skill" [label="no"];
    "Already brainstormed?" -> "Might any skill apply?" [label="yes"];
    "Invoke brainstorming skill" -> "Might any skill apply?";

    "User message received" -> "Might any skill apply?";
    "Might any skill apply?" -> "Invoke Skill tool" [label="yes, even 1%"];
    "Might any skill apply?" -> "Respond (including clarifications)" [label="definitely not"];
    "Invoke Skill tool" -> "Announce: 'Using [skill] to [purpose]'";
    "Announce: 'Using [skill] to [purpose]'" -> "Has checklist?";
    "Has checklist?" -> "Create TodoWrite todo per item" [label="yes"];
    "Has checklist?" -> "Follow skill exactly" [label="no"];
    "Create TodoWrite todo per item" -> "Follow skill exactly";
}
```

## Red Flags

These thoughts mean STOPâ€”you're rationalizing:

| Thought | Reality |
|---------|---------|
| "This is just a simple question" | Questions are tasks. Check for skills. |
| "I need more context first" | Skill check comes BEFORE clarifying questions. |
| "Let me explore the codebase first" | Skills tell you HOW to explore. Check first. |
| "I can check git/files quickly" | Files lack conversation context. Check for skills. |
| "Let me gather information first" | Skills tell you HOW to gather information. |
| "This doesn't need a formal skill" | If a skill exists, use it. |
| "I remember this skill" | Skills evolve. Read current version. |
| "This doesn't count as a task" | Action = task. Check for skills. |
| "The skill is overkill" | Simple things become complex. Use it. |
| "I'll just do this one thing first" | Check BEFORE doing anything. |
| "This feels productive" | Undisciplined action wastes time. Skills prevent this. |
| "I know what that means" | Knowing the concept â‰  using the skill. Invoke it. |

## Skill Priority

When multiple skills could apply, use this order:

1. **Process skills first** (brainstorming, debugging) - these determine HOW to approach the task
2. **Implementation skills second** (frontend-design, mcp-builder) - these guide execution

"Let's build X" â†’ brainstorming first, then implementation skills.
"Fix this bug" â†’ debugging first, then domain-specific skills.

## Skill Types

**Rigid** (TDD, debugging): Follow exactly. Don't adapt away discipline.

**Flexible** (patterns): Adapt principles to context.

The skill itself tells you which.

## User Instructions

Instructions say WHAT, not HOW. "Add X" or "Fix Y" doesn't mean skip workflows.

本裁决不以稿件未解决具名公开问题为理由。就现有主张而言，我没有发现一个可立即构成反例的核心公式；有限窗恒等式、更新结构、临界常数及非暴露斜率填充的主要数学构想大体自洽。然而，现稿存在两个彼此独立的问题：


优先权叙述具有实质性错误。 稿件遗漏了与其压力对象直接等价或极为接近的 Erdős 测度、黄金分割 Bernoulli 卷积及其 Lq-谱文献。尤其是，黄金分割 Bernoulli 卷积的正 q 压力公式早于本稿近三十年，而全实 q 扩张及负 q 相变也早已有明确文献记录。现稿关于“一层/有限窗全实压力”和“一阶冻结相变”之新颖性边界因而不能成立。


两个承重证明环节尚未达到 TAMS 可核验标准。 一是 Lemma 5.7 对 Stern–Brocot/Knauf 压力的规范化转移；二是 Theorem 5.12 中参数一致的多元光滑点渐近及无界代价截断。前者影响 Theorem 5.8 及其后大部分热力学结论，后者影响有限素数支撑部分。


即使上述问题全部修复，我仍不认为修订稿达到 TAMS 的原创性与影响范围门槛。最合适的具体投稿对象是 Journal of Number Theory，条件是重新界定主定理、充分压缩转移性材料并补全证明。

一、优先权与新颖性
1. 被遗漏的并非“邻近文献”，而是同一有限计数对象的既有理论
稿件目前将主要依赖线描述为 Kocábová–Masáková–Pelantová 的极值分类、Weinstein 的自由幺半群以及 Stern–Brocot/Knauf 分母压力，并据此把全实有限窗压力、冻结角点和一层结果列为本文新增内容。 这一依赖图遗漏了下列决定性文献线：


Sidorov–Vershik 已将由前 m 个 Fibonacci 权重产生的表示频数 fm​(k) 作为 Fibonacci graph 的顶点频数，并明确指出归一化质量 2−mfm​(k) 给出 Erdős 测度的有限逼近。其 fm​(k) 与本文的有限 Fibonacci 子集和系数仅有索引约定上的平移，而不是另一种计数函数。arXiv+1


Hu 在 TAMS 上研究了黄金分割 Bernoulli 卷积的局部维数；这至少是本文所谓“归一化对数重数”应当直接比较的早期来源。美国数学学会


Lau–Ngai 已在 1998 年给出黄金分割 Bernoulli 卷积的 Lq-谱公式，适用全部 q>0。EUDML+1


Feng 后来把黄金分割情形的显式 Lq-谱从 q>0 扩张到 q∈R，并指出在负 q 处存在不可微相变；Feng–Olivier 又把 Erdős 测度明确列为具有相变的弱 Gibbs 测度实例。arXiv+2科学直通车+2


因此，稿件不能再把 Bernoulli 卷积理论仅视为宽泛的“相关工作”。这是与本文有限表示频数、矩压力及 Legendre 对偶直接相连的主文献线。
2. 正温压力方程与 Lau–Ngai 公式之间存在直接规范化等价
本文的更新字母由分母 q 和代价 c(p/q) 参数化，并以
p/q∑​qte−P(t)c(p/q)=1
定义正压力分支。 
Lau–Ngai 的公式使用长度 2k+3 的块、矩阵分母
QJ​=[1  1]PJ​(11​)
以及质量
cJ​=2−(2k+3)QJ​,
并由相应更新方程确定 τμ​(t)。数学与计算机科学研究所
按两文公式作直接换元，在完成块记号与负连分数字母的标准索引平移后得到
P(t)=tlog2−τμ​(t)logφ,t>0.
这是公式层面的仿射规范化，而非主题相似性：QJ​ 对应本文的字母分母，2k+3 对应本文的字母代价。作者有责任在正文中完整核实这一字典；在完成该核实以前，Theorem 5.8 和 Theorem 6.1 的正实分支不能作为新压力定理陈述。
Feng 的后续工作又明确记录了同一黄金分割 Lq-谱已经扩张至全部实 q，并在负参数处具有不可微点。arXiv 因而，现稿至少不能再主张下列概念性结论本身是新的：


全实参数压力的存在；


正参数分支的隐式压力方程；


负参数区域出现线性或冻结相；


负参数处的一阶相变；


由压力 Legendre 变换控制的多重分形结构。


仍可能构成真正新增内容的是：


相变点被精确识别为
ζ(σ0​)ζ(σ0​−1)​=2;


临界配分和的线性常数 2/μC​ 与 4/μC​；


临界 Gibbs 律的均匀共存极限；


在均匀有限层概率意义下、包括非暴露斜率的完整 LDP；


联合“生成代价—对数重数”LDP；


有限窗恒等式及其严格有限模型解释。


但这些项目是否已经隐含在 Feng、Feng–Olivier 或相关弱 Gibbs/有限型测度理论中，现稿没有进行任何逐项比较。不能以检索未发现同标题定理代替这一比较。
3. 对其他既有结果的归属
稿件对 Kocábová–Masáková–Pelantová 的极值及全部极大整数的归属基本准确：本文的新内容是显式区间/剩余类转移，而非极值分类本身。正文对此已有较清楚的说明。
对 Weinstein 的归属总体正确，但仍应进一步收缩措辞。自由生成元幺半群、乘子乘法性、层轨道及生成轨道 Dirichlet 级数均为既有结果；本文的
k≤x∑​Ψ(k)∼σ0​κxσ0​​
本质上是已知 Dirichlet 级数加 Wiener–Ikehara 的应用，而非新的轨道结构定理。Weinstein 的公开摘要已经包含自由幺半群、最终层稳定和 Dirichlet 生成函数。arXiv
Sanna 已对每个正整数 p 证明 Fibonacci 分拆幂和的指数增长，并研究 p→∞ 的零温端点。arXiv 因而，正整数点上的压力存在性和指数率应明确称为既有结果；本文最多是在另一路径上恢复这些点，并把参数扩展到实轴。但正实参数的扩展又已经受到 Lau–Ngai 理论覆盖，故“从整数矩到全实压力”不能作为主要原创性叙事。
4. 优先权裁决
现稿的文献审计结论不成立。问题并不是 MathSciNet 访问不完整，而是已经存在数篇直接命中的经典论文，却未列入参考文献。稿件目前关于贡献边界的表述具有误导性，属于足以阻止发表的优先权问题。
作者必须新增一份逐定理比较表，至少包括：
本文结论既有对应对象精确规范化真正新增部分P(t)Erdős 测度的 Lq-谱P(t)=tlog2−τμ​(t)logφ是否仅剩临界点的算术识别冻结角点负 q 相变参数与符号转换精确 σ0​、单侧导数常数是否新增标量 LDP多重分形/奇异性谱层计数与网格质量的转换均匀层概率 LDP 与非暴露局部下界临界 Gibbs 律弱 Gibbs 相变理论有限尺度归一化线性常数及均匀混合律正整数矩Sanna 的幂和层端点与全区间换算无或仅常数级改进
在这份比较完成以前，不能判断压力和标量 LDP 中究竟有多少是真正新的。

二、数学正确性
总体判断
我没有发现 Theorems 3.6、3.9、4.1 和 4.2 中的显式有限恒等式存在明显错误。反转—补映射、仿射置换、有限乘积截断以及双区间分解的证明链可以跟随。   极值公式则确实是从既有极值分类作规范与区间转换，证明也与这一定位一致。
临界更新定理、线性常数和非暴露斜率填充的总体策略同样具有可信性。不过，以下环节尚不足以使审稿人确认全文正确。
1. Lemma 5.7 的 Stern–Brocot/Knauf 规范化是承重缺口
Lemma 5.7 声称负连分数代价层
ad​(s)=d(p/q)=d∑​q−s
与 Knauf 的“新分母层”精确一致，并据此读取 Stern–Brocot 压力；在 s<0 时又通过矩阵词的桥接不等式延伸该结论。
当前证明缺少一个独立、可逐项核验的规范化引理。至少需要明确证明：


规范负连分数 (a1​,…,ar​) 与 Knauf 新分母词之间的双射；


本文 d(p/q)=∑(ai​−1) 与文献“深度”的精确索引关系；


负 continuant 分母与矩阵范数或 Stern–Brocot 分母的逐词相等；


为什么文献压力参数恰为 s/2，而本文半径中又出现额外的 1/2；


边界词及深度 0,1 是否被重复或遗漏。


目前仅以“exactly”以及若干文献方程编号连接这几个约定，不足以排除一位移或二倍因子错误。由于 Theorem 5.8 的收敛半径、根分类、压力存在性和正压力区间均依赖该引理，此项是对现稿主定理链致命、但原则上可修复的证明缺口。
足以消除异议的修订是：给出完整规范化引理、逐词证明以及前四至五个代价层的显式对照表。单纯增加数值回归不能替代这一证明。
2. Theorem 5.12 的多元解析组合证明过度压缩
Theorem 5.12 关于定向鞍点、自由词长度、条件代价律及严格非活跃窗转移的代数部分基本可信。 但证明中从“光滑严格极小点”直接转到“usual analytic quasi-powers estimate”，以及随后对无界代价使用指点系数比值，均缺少足够精确的定理陈述。
至少需要补充：


带字母数标记的扰动奇异簇在复参数邻域内的解析性；


分子、分母系数渐近的统一误差项；


指点一次后 (1−A)−2 与 (1−A)−1 系数比的精确一阶常数；


当 ρ 处于紧子集且 nN​−Nρ=O(1) 时的参数一致性；


截断代价 C∧M 后先令 N→∞、再令 M→∞ 的一致可积性；


整数取整与方向晶格对局部系数公式的影响。


“协方差正定”和“严格极小”本身不足以自动给出全部这些结论。足以消除异议的修订是给出一个适用于本文有理函数族的参数一致光滑点系数引理，并把无界代价部分写成独立的截断—尾控制命题。另一种可接受处理是从主稿删除 Theorem 5.12；这不会破坏压力、临界律和 LDP 主线。
3. Weinstein 轨道公式需要固定版本与约定
统一早层计数界的思路合理，但证明多次使用下列未被独立列出的事实：


每个生成元位于指定的奇首指标 sheet；


其初块参数恰为 1；


在该子类上 L(τg)=L(g)+1，而不只是一般公式中的 L(g)±1；


四个分支互不相交，并且在端点层 j=2k 恰好全部出现。


这些事实散布在对 Weinstein 公式的解释中。  由于 Weinstein 的公开摘要仍把稳定范围表述为 i>2k，正文应固定所用版本，并把 i=2k 的加强明确列为本文引理，而不是在归属说明中含混处理。arXiv+1
这一问题可通过一页左右的“轨道约定与端点引理”解决；其本身不改变期刊层级裁决。
4. LDP 证明需要若干形式化补缀
标量 LDP 中的非暴露斜率填充是本文较有价值的部分，核心注入构造可以跟随。 但正文仍需修正下列细节：


Theorem 6.1 在定义 ρ=α/v 前，应先明确选择 t 使 v=P′(t)>α。当前书写先固定任意 t>tc​，对 α>vc​ 时并不自动保证 ρ<1；后文取 t↓tα​ 的意图虽然可以修复这一点，但逻辑次序应重写。


联合 LDP 的低奖励 suffix 对奇偶剩余代价的构造是正确方向，但应把 r=6,7,… 的小值、重复字母许可及取整误差统一列成一个填充引理，而不是留在段落性说明中。


从内部点下界推到 α+​ 及联合有效域边界的论证，应明确引用一个相对拓扑开集的逼近引理；当前“continuity supplies the lower bound”的表达过于简略。


这些是可修复的严谨性问题，不足以推出 LDP 结论为假。
5. 摘要边界与正文存在两处明确不一致
用户提供的主张边界中写作 Cm​(1)=2，而正文只在 m≥1 时如此；m=0 时显然 C0​(1)=1。正文的范围是正确的。
此外，摘要中给出 I2k+1​,I2k​,J2k​ 而未注明定义范围；正文实际要求前者 k≥5，后两者 k≥7。 这些范围必须进入所有主定理摘要和投稿信，不能依靠异常表隐式修补。
6. 紧算子“不存在性”命题在其假设下成立，但意义被放大
Proposition 4.6 在“固定 Banach 空间、算子范数解析、紧算子、正代数单孤立特征值、局部一致谱隙”这一整组强假设下，利用冻结半轴排除全局解析特征值表示，逻辑上没有明显问题。
但它并不排除：


参数依赖的函数空间；


非紧转移算子；


可数状态 Ruelle 算子；


在相变点失去谱隙的算子族；


分相定义的算子表示。


故该命题不应被宣传为对“转移算子方法”或“谱表示”的普遍障碍。建议降为短推论或备注。此项为表述问题，不影响主数学。

三、TAMS 发表门槛
1. 在正确归属以后，剩余增量如何评价
若按上述文献重新归属，则稿件的内容大致分为四层：


既有核心框架：Erdős 测度的有限 Fibonacci 表示频数、黄金分割 Bernoulli 卷积的 Lq-谱、负参数相变、多重分形 Legendre 结构。


既有算术结构：Weinstein 自由幺半群及 Dirichlet 级数、Kocábová–Masáková–Pelantová 极值分类、Sanna 正整数矩。


本文可信的新转移：仿射置换、分拆差、双区间恒等式、全部极大剩余类的显式转移、早层统一计数界。


本文可能具有实质性的新渐近：临界点的 zeta 方程、2m/μC​ 与 4m/μC​ 常数、均匀共存律、非暴露斜率局部下界、二维 LDP 及严格非活跃有限素数支撑转移。


第四层并非微不足道；其中临界有限尺寸常数和非暴露斜率构造具有发表价值。但它们是在一个已被充分发展的黄金分割 Bernoulli 卷积压力体系内作出的精确化，而不是建立了新的广泛机制。有限窗部分又主要是将经典 R(n) 的已有结构显式搬运到一个折叠模型。
2. 为什么仍不足以达到 TAMS
TAMS 并不要求每篇文章解决具名公开问题，但通常要求主结果在其领域中具有明显的结构性推进、较宽的适用范围或足够深的单一核心。现稿在重新归属后存在以下层级问题：


标题和摘要中的第一主轴——“all-real pressure and first-order freezing transition”——在概念和公式上已经有重要先例；


极值分类本身完全外来；


Tauberian 轨道律、临界更新以及大偏差暴露点部分大量使用标准转移；


有限素数支撑定理与主压力/LDP 线联系较弱，并且证明尚不完整；


篇幅中相当比例用于重复陈述一层与两层版本、归属性说明及有限转移，而非新增数学深度。


因此，即使证明完全修复、优先权完全改写，我预计 TAMS 的最终裁决仍会是拒稿。这个判断与是否攻克邻近开放问题无关。
3. 最合适的具体期刊
Journal of Number Theory 是最合适的投稿对象。其理由是：


主对象是经典 Fibonacci partition function；


分拆恒等式、极值转移、Dirichlet 级数和临界算术常数均属数论与解析组合方向；


Chow–Slattery 与 Chow–Jones 关于同一函数的直接前作也发表于该刊，稿件可在同一文献语境中被准确评价。


但这不是将现稿原样改投即可。适合 JNT 的版本仍须完成优先权重写、证明修复和结构压缩。

四、必要修改及其对裁决的影响
等级异议足以消除异议的修改对裁决的影响致命遗漏 Sidorov–Vershik、Hu、Lau–Ngai、Feng、Feng–Olivier增补文献；证明有限表示频数与 Erdős 测度有限质量的精确字典；给出 P 与 τμ​ 的参数转换可恢复可信优先权叙述；不改变 TAMS 拒稿致命把全实压力及负参数相变整体列为本文新结果逐定理比较 Feng 2005 的全实谱与本文 P；明确究竟只有临界点位置、常数或有限层概率定理是新的若全部压力结论已知，稿件须大幅缩短；若精确临界结果新，可支持 JNT致命摘要、引言和贡献边界对新颖性作过度陈述重写摘要、Theorems A/F/G 的导言以及 §1.2；不得继续使用“first determination of all-real pressure”一类措辞修复诚信与可审性；不改变 TAMS 裁决必须大修Lemma 5.7 的 Knauf/Stern–Brocot 规范化不完整独立规范化引理、逐词双射、参数因子证明及低层对照若成功，可确认 Theorem 5.8 以后主链；是任何期刊接受的必要条件必须大修Theorem 5.12 的 ACSV 与尾截断证明不足参数一致的光滑点系数引理、指点系数比、统一尾估计；或删除该定理修复后可保留有限素数支撑部分；对 TAMS 层级无实质改变必须大修Weinstein 轨道端点与版本约定不够明确固定引用版本；列出 sheet、θ=1、L(τg) 和 j=2k 端点引理提高可核验性；不改变期刊裁决必须大修标量及联合 LDP 的若干量词和边界步骤过简明确 P′(t)>α 的参数选择；独立 suffix 填充引理；补充有效域边界逼近引理足以使 LDP 证明达到发表标准；不改变 TAMS 层级必须大修主张摘要与正文范围不一致把 m≥1、k≥5、k≥7 等范围写入所有定理摘要消除形式错误可选但强烈建议一层与两层结果大量重复选定一条主定理脊柱；另一版本改为短推论提高论文的新颖性密度可选紧算子排除命题被赋予过强解释降为推论或备注，并明确未排除非紧、可数状态或失谱隙表示改善表述，不影响核心可选极大剩余类的长表占据主文主文保留转换定理，将异常表和机器核验移入附录或可归档补充材料改善结构，不影响正确性

最终裁决


数学正确性：有限恒等式与更新/LDP 主构想大体可信，未发现明确反例；但 Lemma 5.7 和 Theorem 5.12 的现有证明不足，故不能确认全文已证明。


新颖性与优先权：当前叙述不准确。黄金分割 Bernoulli 卷积的 Lq-谱、全实扩张及负参数相变是必须正面处理的既有成果；它们与本文压力之间存在直接的有限频数与参数规范化关系。


TAMS 门槛：不达到。即使全部技术问题修复，经过正确归属后剩余贡献仍是一个专门对象上的精确临界化、有限窗转移与 LDP 补全，而非 TAMS 所通常要求的结构性推进。


适当去向：经实质性重写后，Journal of Number Theory。若作者证明精确 zeta 临界点、临界线性常数、均匀共存律及非暴露斜率 LDP 均不包含于既有 Erdős 测度理论，则这些结果足以形成一篇有价值的 JNT 论文；若进一步比较表明这些结论也已隐含于 Feng 或 Feng–Olivier 的框架，则现有稿件还须显著缩小主张并重新评估是否足以构成独立长文。

---
name: writing-plans
description: Use when you have a spec or requirements for a multi-step task, before touching code
---

# Writing Plans

## Overview

Write comprehensive implementation plans assuming the engineer has zero context for our codebase and questionable taste. Document everything they need to know: which files to touch for each task, code, testing, docs they might need to check, how to test it. Give them the whole plan as bite-sized tasks. DRY. YAGNI. TDD. Frequent commits.

Assume they are a skilled developer, but know almost nothing about our toolset or problem domain. Assume they don't know good test design very well.

**Announce at start:** "I'm using the writing-plans skill to create the implementation plan."

**Context:** This should be run in a dedicated worktree (created by brainstorming skill).

**Save plans to:** `docs/superpowers/plans/YYYY-MM-DD-<feature-name>.md`
- (User preferences for plan location override this default)

## Scope Check

If the spec covers multiple independent subsystems, it should have been broken into sub-project specs during brainstorming. If it wasn't, suggest breaking this into separate plans — one per subsystem. Each plan should produce working, testable software on its own.

## File Structure

Before defining tasks, map out which files will be created or modified and what each one is responsible for. This is where decomposition decisions get locked in.

- Design units with clear boundaries and well-defined interfaces. Each file should have one clear responsibility.
- You reason best about code you can hold in context at once, and your edits are more reliable when files are focused. Prefer smaller, focused files over large ones that do too much.
- Files that change together should live together. Split by responsibility, not by technical layer.
- In existing codebases, follow established patterns. If the codebase uses large files, don't unilaterally restructure - but if a file you're modifying has grown unwieldy, including a split in the plan is reasonable.

This structure informs the task decomposition. Each task should produce self-contained changes that make sense independently.

## Bite-Sized Task Granularity

**Each step is one action (2-5 minutes):**
- "Write the failing test" - step
- "Run it to make sure it fails" - step
- "Implement the minimal code to make the test pass" - step
- "Run the tests and make sure they pass" - step
- "Commit" - step

## Plan Document Header

**Every plan MUST start with this header:**

```markdown
# [Feature Name] Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** [One sentence describing what this builds]

**Architecture:** [2-3 sentences about approach]

**Tech Stack:** [Key technologies/libraries]

---
```

## Task Structure

````markdown
### Task N: [Component Name]

**Files:**
- Create: `exact/path/to/file.py`
- Modify: `exact/path/to/existing.py:123-145`
- Test: `tests/exact/path/to/test.py`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

```python
def test_specific_behavior():
    result = function(input)
    assert result == expected
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run test to verify it fails**

Run: `pytest tests/path/test.py::test_name -v`
Expected: FAIL with "function not defined"

- [ ] **Step 3: Write minimal implementation**

```python
def function(input):
    return expected
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Run test to verify it passes**

Run: `pytest tests/path/test.py::test_name -v`
Expected: PASS

- [ ] **Step 5: Commit**

```bash
git add tests/path/test.py src/path/file.py
git commit -m "feat: add specific feature"
```
````

## No Placeholders

Every step must contain the actual content an engineer needs. These are **plan failures** — never write them:
- "TBD", "TODO", "implement later", "fill in details"
- "Add appropriate error handling" / "add validation" / "handle edge cases"
- "Write tests for the above" (without actual test code)
- "Similar to Task N" (repeat the code — the engineer may be reading tasks out of order)
- Steps that describe what to do without showing how (code blocks required for code steps)
- References to types, functions, or methods not defined in any task

## Remember
- Exact file paths always
- Complete code in every step — if a step changes code, show the code
- Exact commands with expected output
- DRY, YAGNI, TDD, frequent commits

## Self-Review

After writing the complete plan, look at the spec with fresh eyes and check the plan against it. This is a checklist you run yourself — not a subagent dispatch.

**1. Spec coverage:** Skim each section/requirement in the spec. Can you point to a task that implements it? List any gaps.

**2. Placeholder scan:** Search your plan for red flags — any of the patterns from the "No Placeholders" section above. Fix them.

**3. Type consistency:** Do the types, method signatures, and property names you used in later tasks match what you defined in earlier tasks? A function called `clearLayers()` in Task 3 but `clearFullLayers()` in Task 7 is a bug.

If you find issues, fix them inline. No need to re-review — just fix and move on. If you find a spec requirement with no task, add the task.

## Execution Handoff

After saving the plan, offer execution choice:

**"Plan complete and saved to `docs/superpowers/plans/<filename>.md`. Two execution options:**

**1. Subagent-Driven (recommended)** - I dispatch a fresh subagent per task, review between tasks, fast iteration

**2. Inline Execution** - Execute tasks in this session using executing-plans, batch execution with checkpoints

**Which approach?"**

**If Subagent-Driven chosen:**
- **REQUIRED SUB-SKILL:** Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development
- Fresh subagent per task + two-stage review

**If Inline Execution chosen:**
- **REQUIRED SUB-SKILL:** Use superpowers:executing-plans
- Batch execution with checkpoints for review

name: brainstorming
description: "You MUST use this before any creative work - creating features, building components, adding functionality, or modifying behavior. Explores user intent, requirements and design before implementation."
---

# Brainstorming Ideas Into Designs

Help turn ideas into fully formed designs and specs through natural collaborative dialogue.

Start by understanding the current project context, then ask questions one at a time to refine the idea. Once you understand what you're building, present the design and get user approval.

<HARD-GATE>
Do NOT invoke any implementation skill, write any code, scaffold any project, or take any implementation action until you have presented a design and the user has approved it. This applies to EVERY project regardless of perceived simplicity.
</HARD-GATE>

## Anti-Pattern: "This Is Too Simple To Need A Design"

Every project goes through this process. A todo list, a single-function utility, a config change — all of them. "Simple" projects are where unexamined assumptions cause the most wasted work. The design can be short (a few sentences for truly simple projects), but you MUST present it and get approval.

## Checklist

You MUST create a task for each of these items and complete them in order:

1. **Explore project context** — check files, docs, recent commits
2. **Offer visual companion** (if topic will involve visual questions) — this is its own message, not combined with a clarifying question. See the Visual Companion section below.
3. **Ask clarifying questions** — one at a time, understand purpose/constraints/success criteria
4. **Propose 2-3 approaches** — with trade-offs and your recommendation
5. **Present design** — in sections scaled to their complexity, get user approval after each section
6. **Write design doc** — save to `docs/superpowers/specs/YYYY-MM-DD-<topic>-design.md` and commit
7. **Spec self-review** — quick inline check for placeholders, contradictions, ambiguity, scope (see below)
8. **User reviews written spec** — ask user to review the spec file before proceeding
9. **Transition to implementation** — invoke writing-plans skill to create implementation plan

## Process Flow

```dot
digraph brainstorming {
    "Explore project context" [shape=box];
    "Visual questions ahead?" [shape=diamond];
    "Offer Visual Companion\n(own message, no other content)" [shape=box];
    "Ask clarifying questions" [shape=box];
    "Propose 2-3 approaches" [shape=box];
    "Present design sections" [shape=box];
    "User approves design?" [shape=diamond];
    "Write design doc" [shape=box];
    "Spec self-review\n(fix inline)" [shape=box];
    "User reviews spec?" [shape=diamond];
    "Invoke writing-plans skill" [shape=doublecircle];

    "Explore project context" -> "Visual questions ahead?";
    "Visual questions ahead?" -> "Offer Visual Companion\n(own message, no other content)" [label="yes"];
    "Visual questions ahead?" -> "Ask clarifying questions" [label="no"];
    "Offer Visual Companion\n(own message, no other content)" -> "Ask clarifying questions";
    "Ask clarifying questions" -> "Propose 2-3 approaches";
    "Propose 2-3 approaches" -> "Present design sections";
    "Present design sections" -> "User approves design?";
    "User approves design?" -> "Present design sections" [label="no, revise"];
    "User approves design?" -> "Write design doc" [label="yes"];
    "Write design doc" -> "Spec self-review\n(fix inline)";
    "Spec self-review\n(fix inline)" -> "User reviews spec?";
    "User reviews spec?" -> "Write design doc" [label="changes requested"];
    "User reviews spec?" -> "Invoke writing-plans skill" [label="approved"];
}
```

**The terminal state is invoking writing-plans.** Do NOT invoke frontend-design, mcp-builder, or any other implementation skill. The ONLY skill you invoke after brainstorming is writing-plans.

## The Process

**Understanding the idea:**

- Check out the current project state first (files, docs, recent commits)
- Before asking detailed questions, assess scope: if the request describes multiple independent subsystems (e.g., "build a platform with chat, file storage, billing, and analytics"), flag this immediately. Don't spend questions refining details of a project that needs to be decomposed first.
- If the project is too large for a single spec, help the user decompose into sub-projects: what are the independent pieces, how do they relate, what order should they be built? Then brainstorm the first sub-project through the normal design flow. Each sub-project gets its own spec → plan → implementation cycle.
- For appropriately-scoped projects, ask questions one at a time to refine the idea
- Prefer multiple choice questions when possible, but open-ended is fine too
- Only one question per message - if a topic needs more exploration, break it into multiple questions
- Focus on understanding: purpose, constraints, success criteria

**Exploring approaches:**

- Propose 2-3 different approaches with trade-offs
- Present options conversationally with your recommendation and reasoning
- Lead with your recommended option and explain why

**Presenting the design:**

- Once you believe you understand what you're building, present the design
- Scale each section to its complexity: a few sentences if straightforward, up to 200-300 words if nuanced
- Ask after each section whether it looks right so far
- Cover: architecture, components, data flow, error handling, testing
- Be ready to go back and clarify if something doesn't make sense

**Design for isolation and clarity:**

- Break the system into smaller units that each have one clear purpose, communicate through well-defined interfaces, and can be understood and tested independently
- For each unit, you should be able to answer: what does it do, how do you use it, and what does it depend on?
- Can someone understand what a unit does without reading its internals? Can you change the internals without breaking consumers? If not, the boundaries need work.
- Smaller, well-bounded units are also easier for you to work with - you reason better about code you can hold in context at once, and your edits are more reliable when files are focused. When a file grows large, that's often a signal that it's doing too much.

**Working in existing codebases:**

- Explore the current structure before proposing changes. Follow existing patterns.
- Where existing code has problems that affect the work (e.g., a file that's grown too large, unclear boundaries, tangled responsibilities), include targeted improvements as part of the design - the way a good developer improves code they're working in.
- Don't propose unrelated refactoring. Stay focused on what serves the current goal.

## After the Design

**Documentation:**

- Write the validated design (spec) to `docs/superpowers/specs/YYYY-MM-DD-<topic>-design.md`
  - (User preferences for spec location override this default)
- Use elements-of-style:writing-clearly-and-concisely skill if available
- Commit the design document to git

**Spec Self-Review:**
After writing the spec document, look at it with fresh eyes:

1. **Placeholder scan:** Any "TBD", "TODO", incomplete sections, or vague requirements? Fix them.
2. **Internal consistency:** Do any sections contradict each other? Does the architecture match the feature descriptions?
3. **Scope check:** Is this focused enough for a single implementation plan, or does it need decomposition?
4. **Ambiguity check:** Could any requirement be interpreted two different ways? If so, pick one and make it explicit.

Fix any issues inline. No need to re-review — just fix and move on.

**User Review Gate:**
After the spec review loop passes, ask the user to review the written spec before proceeding:

> "Spec written and committed to `<path>`. Please review it and let me know if you want to make any changes before we start writing out the implementation plan."

Wait for the user's response. If they request changes, make them and re-run the spec review loop. Only proceed once the user approves.

**Implementation:**

- Invoke the writing-plans skill to create a detailed implementation plan
- Do NOT invoke any other skill. writing-plans is the next step.

## Key Principles

- **One question at a time** - Don't overwhelm with multiple questions
- **Multiple choice preferred** - Easier to answer than open-ended when possible
- **YAGNI ruthlessly** - Remove unnecessary features from all designs
- **Explore alternatives** - Always propose 2-3 approaches before settling
- **Incremental validation** - Present design, get approval before moving on
- **Be flexible** - Go back and clarify when something doesn't make sense

## Visual Companion

A browser-based companion for showing mockups, diagrams, and visual options during brainstorming. Available as a tool — not a mode. Accepting the companion means it's available for questions that benefit from visual treatment; it does NOT mean every question goes through the browser.

**Offering the companion:** When you anticipate that upcoming questions will involve visual content (mockups, layouts, diagrams), offer it once for consent:
> "Some of what we're working on might be easier to explain if I can show it to you in a web browser. I can put together mockups, diagrams, comparisons, and other visuals as we go. This feature is still new and can be token-intensive. Want to try it? (Requires opening a local URL)"

**This offer MUST be its own message.** Do not combine it with clarifying questions, context summaries, or any other content. The message should contain ONLY the offer above and nothing else. Wait for the user's response before continuing. If they decline, proceed with text-only brainstorming.

**Per-question decision:** Even after the user accepts, decide FOR EACH QUESTION whether to use the browser or the terminal. The test: **would the user understand this better by seeing it than reading it?**

- **Use the browser** for content that IS visual — mockups, wireframes, layout comparisons, architecture diagrams, side-by-side visual designs
- **Use the terminal** for content that is text — requirements questions, conceptual choices, tradeoff lists, A/B/C/D text options, scope decisions

A question about a UI topic is not automatically a visual question. "What does personality mean in this context?" is a conceptual question — use the terminal. "Which wizard layout works better?" is a visual question — use the browser.

If they agree to the companion, read the detailed guide before proceeding:
`skills/brainstorming/visual-companion.md`

name: verification-before-completion
description: Use when about to claim work is complete, fixed, or passing, before committing or creating PRs - requires running verification commands and confirming output before making any success claims; evidence before assertions always
---

# Verification Before Completion

## Overview

Claiming work is complete without verification is dishonesty, not efficiency.

**Core principle:** Evidence before claims, always.

**Violating the letter of this rule is violating the spirit of this rule.**

## The Iron Law

```
NO COMPLETION CLAIMS WITHOUT FRESH VERIFICATION EVIDENCE
```

If you haven't run the verification command in this message, you cannot claim it passes.

## The Gate Function

```
BEFORE claiming any status or expressing satisfaction:

1. IDENTIFY: What command proves this claim?
2. RUN: Execute the FULL command (fresh, complete)
3. READ: Full output, check exit code, count failures
4. VERIFY: Does output confirm the claim?
   - If NO: State actual status with evidence
   - If YES: State claim WITH evidence
5. ONLY THEN: Make the claim

Skip any step = lying, not verifying
```

## Common Failures

| Claim | Requires | Not Sufficient |
|-------|----------|----------------|
| Tests pass | Test command output: 0 failures | Previous run, "should pass" |
| Linter clean | Linter output: 0 errors | Partial check, extrapolation |
| Build succeeds | Build command: exit 0 | Linter passing, logs look good |
| Bug fixed | Test original symptom: passes | Code changed, assumed fixed |
| Regression test works | Red-green cycle verified | Test passes once |
| Agent completed | VCS diff shows changes | Agent reports "success" |
| Requirements met | Line-by-line checklist | Tests passing |

## Red Flags - STOP

- Using "should", "probably", "seems to"
- Expressing satisfaction before verification ("Great!", "Perfect!", "Done!", etc.)
- About to commit/push/PR without verification
- Trusting agent success reports
- Relying on partial verification
- Thinking "just this once"
- Tired and wanting work over
- **ANY wording implying success without having run verification**

## Rationalization Prevention

| Excuse | Reality |
|--------|---------|
| "Should work now" | RUN the verification |
| "I'm confident" | Confidence ≠ evidence |
| "Just this once" | No exceptions |
| "Linter passed" | Linter ≠ compiler |
| "Agent said success" | Verify independently |
| "I'm tired" | Exhaustion ≠ excuse |
| "Partial check is enough" | Partial proves nothing |
| "Different words so rule doesn't apply" | Spirit over letter |

## Key Patterns

**Tests:**
```
✅ [Run test command] [See: 34/34 pass] "All tests pass"
❌ "Should pass now" / "Looks correct"
```

**Regression tests (TDD Red-Green):**
```
✅ Write → Run (pass) → Revert fix → Run (MUST FAIL) → Restore → Run (pass)
❌ "I've written a regression test" (without red-green verification)
```

**Build:**
```
✅ [Run build] [See: exit 0] "Build passes"
❌ "Linter passed" (linter doesn't check compilation)
```

**Requirements:**
```
✅ Re-read plan → Create checklist → Verify each → Report gaps or completion
❌ "Tests pass, phase complete"
```

**Agent delegation:**
```
✅ Agent reports success → Check VCS diff → Verify changes → Report actual state
❌ Trust agent report
```

## Why This Matters

From 24 failure memories:
- your human partner said "I don't believe you" - trust broken
- Undefined functions shipped - would crash
- Missing requirements shipped - incomplete features
- Time wasted on false completion → redirect → rework
- Violates: "Honesty is a core value. If you lie, you'll be replaced."

## When To Apply

**ALWAYS before:**
- ANY variation of success/completion claims
- ANY expression of satisfaction
- ANY positive statement about work state
- Committing, PR creation, task completion
- Moving to next task
- Delegating to agents

**Rule applies to:**
- Exact phrases
- Paraphrases and synonyms
- Implications of success
- ANY communication suggesting completion/correctness

## The Bottom Line

**No shortcuts for verification.**

Run the command. Read the output. THEN claim the result.

This is non-negotiable.

# PDF Skill

## When to use
- Read or review PDF content where layout and visuals matter.
- Create PDFs programmatically with reliable formatting.
- Validate final rendering before delivery.

## Workflow
1. Prefer visual review: render PDF pages to PNGs and inspect them.
   - Use `pdftoppm` if available.
   - If unavailable, install Poppler or ask the user to review the output locally.
2. Use `reportlab` to generate PDFs when creating new documents.
3. Use `pdfplumber` (or `pypdf`) for text extraction and quick checks; do not rely on it for layout fidelity.
4. After each meaningful update, re-render pages and verify alignment, spacing, and legibility.

## Temp and output conventions
- Use `tmp/pdfs/` for intermediate files; delete when done.
- Write final artifacts under `output/pdf/` when working in this repo.
- Keep filenames stable and descriptive.

## Dependencies (install if missing)
Prefer `uv` for dependency management.

Python packages:
```
uv pip install reportlab pdfplumber pypdf
```
If `uv` is unavailable:
```
python3 -m pip install reportlab pdfplumber pypdf
```
System tools (for rendering):
```
# macOS (Homebrew)
brew install poppler

# Ubuntu/Debian
sudo apt-get install -y poppler-utils
```

If installation isn't possible in this environment, tell the user which dependency is missing and how to install it locally.

## Environment
No required environment variables.

## Rendering command
```
pdftoppm -png $INPUT_PDF $OUTPUT_PREFIX
```

## Quality expectations
- Maintain polished visual design: consistent typography, spacing, margins, and section hierarchy.
- Avoid rendering issues: clipped text, overlapping elements, broken tables, black squares, or unreadable glyphs.
- Charts, tables, and images must be sharp, aligned, and clearly labeled.
- Use ASCII hyphens only. Avoid U+2011 (non-breaking hyphen) and other Unicode dashes.
- Citations and references must be human-readable; never leave tool tokens or placeholder strings.

## Final checks
- Do not deliver until the latest PNG inspection shows zero visual or formatting defects.
- Confirm headers/footers, page numbering, and section transitions look polished.
- Keep intermediate files organized or remove them after final approval.

\title[Fibonacci partition thermodynamics]{%
  Fibonacci Partition Thermodynamics and\\
  Finite-Window Zeckendorf Fibers}

\author{Haobo Ma}
\address{AELF PTE LTD., \#14-02, Marina Bay Financial Centre Tower 1,
8 Marina Blvd, Singapore 018981, Singapore}
\email{auric@aelf.io}

\address{National University of Singapore, Singapore}
\email{e1327962@u.nus.edu}
\hypersetup{
  pdftitle={Fibonacci Partition Thermodynamics and Finite-Window Zeckendorf Fibers},
  pdfauthor={Haobo Ma and Wenlin Zhang},
  pdfsubject={Thermodynamics of the Fibonacci partition function and finite-window fibers},
  pdfkeywords={Fibonacci partition function, Zeckendorf representation, large deviations, pressure, negative-temperature freezing}
}
\date{}
\subjclass[2020]{11B39, 05A17, 60F10}
\keywords{Fibonacci partition function, Zeckendorf representation, large
deviations, pressure, negative-temperature freezing}

\begin{document}

\begin{abstract}
Let \(R(n)\) be the number of partitions of \(n\) into distinct Fibonacci
numbers, with the two unit terms identified.  Its layer moment pressure is an
affine normalization of the \(L^q\)-spectrum of the golden-ratio Bernoulli
convolution.  Lau--Ngai determined the positive-\(q\) spectrum, and
Feng--Olivier and Feng determined the all-real spectrum and its negative
first-order phase transition.  We give the exact finite-count dictionary and
recover that pressure in Fibonacci-partition coordinates.  Our refinements
identify its critical parameter arithmetically by
\(\zeta(\sigma_0-1)/\zeta(\sigma_0)=2\).  At criticality
the one-layer partition sum is asymptotic to \(2m/\mu_C\), and its Gibbs law
converges to a uniform mixture of generating-layer fractions.  Under the
 uniform law on \(\mathcal I_m\), \(m^{-1}\log R(n)\) satisfies a full large
 deviation principle; the rate is affine on the coexistence interval.  Jointly
 with normalized generating cost, it satisfies a two-dimensional large
 deviation principle whose rate surface has an explicit affine coexistence
 face.  The new inputs for these refinements are a weighted renewal identity
for Weinstein's free generators and a parity-free orbit-filling argument for
the nonexposed slopes.

A finite-window Zeckendorf fold provides an exact finite model for the same
spectrum: its fiber list is the list of \(R\) on two consecutive layers.
An affine permutation also identifies each fiber with a finite Fibonacci
subset-sum coefficient and a partition-function difference.  This yields the
 exact largest fibers
 \(D_{2k}=F_{k+2}\) for \(k\ge0\), \(D_{2k+1}=2F_{k+1}\) for \(k\ge1\), and
 \(D_m=D_{m-2}+D_{m-4}\) for \(m\ge6\), including every maximizing residue.  The two-layer
model has the same pressure and large-deviation rate, an exact frozen
Dirichlet limit, and a critical finite-size constant \(4/\mu_C\).  The frozen
half-line also rules out a globally analytic compact-operator representation
 under precise spectral hypotheses.  On every fixed finite prime support we
 retain an exact coefficient generating function; no directional coefficient
 asymptotic is asserted without a parameter-uniform local theorem.
\end{abstract}

 \begin{abstract}

-numbers, with the two unit terms identified.  On the standard Fibonacci layer
-\(\mathcal I_m=[F_{m+1}-1,F_{m+2}-1)\), we determine the all-real moment

+\noindent\textbf{Erd\H{o}s measure and Bernoulli convolutions.}
+Sidorov--Vershik identify normalized finite Fibonacci representation
+frequencies with approximants to the Erd\H{o}s measure
+\cite{SidorovVershik1998}.  Hu studied its golden-ratio local dimensions
+\cite{Hu1997}; Lau--Ngai gave the exact \(L^q\)-spectrum for \(q>0\)
+\cite{LauNgai1998}; Feng--Olivier and Feng gave the complete real spectrum and
+the negative first-order transition \cite{FengOlivier2003,Feng2005}.  These
+are imported results, not merely related background.
+
+If \(\mu\) denotes the golden-ratio Bernoulli convolution and
+\(\tau_\mu(t)\) uses the convention
+\(\tau_\mu(t)=\lim \log\sum_I\mu(I)^t/\log|I|\), then the level masses are
+\(2^{-m}\) times the finite Fibonacci representation counts.  Since the net
+scale is \(\varphi^{-m}\), the exact exponential-rate dictionary is
+\begin{equation}
+ P(t)=t\log2-\tau_\mu(t)\log\varphi.
+ \label{eq:erdos-pressure-dictionary}
+\end{equation}
+Thus the all-real pressure, its positive implicit branch, the negative frozen
+branch, the first-order corner, and the generic Legendre multifractal
+description are prior results.  The present paper adds the exact
+finite-window/fiber correspondences, evaluates Feng's implicit critical
+condition as \(\zeta(\sigma_0-1)/\zeta(\sigma_0)=2\), obtains the critical
+linear constants and uniform coexistence law, and proves the stated
+finite-layer nonexposed-slope lower bounds.
+
+\begin{center}
+\begin{tabular}{p{0.25\textwidth}p{0.31\textwidth}p{0.34\textwidth}}
+\toprule
+Object here & Prior result and normalization & Increment established here\\

+Layer pressure \(P(t)\) & Lau--Ngai for \(t>0\); Feng for all
+\(t\in\mathbb R\); \eqref{eq:erdos-pressure-dictionary} & Exact layer/window
+translation and renewal coordinates\\
+Frozen corner & Feng--Olivier and Feng's negative-\(q\) first-order phase
+transition & Totient/zeta evaluation of the critical point and one-sided
+moment formula\\

+\(2/\mu_C\), \(4/\mu_C\), and the uniform finite-layer coexistence limit\\

+partition-difference, two-layer, and maximizing-residue identities\\

+We use the generation convention in which the first interior fraction
+\(1/2\) has depth \(d=1\).  The denominator form of the same pressure

+ \label{eq:stern-brocot-denominator-convention}
+\end{equation}

+\begin{lemma}[Continued-fraction/Knauf normalization]
+\label{lem:continued-fraction-knauf-normalization}
+For \(d\ge1\), write a composition of \(d\) as
+\(d=e_1+\cdots+e_r\), and put \(a_i=e_i+1\).  Encode the composition by the
+word \(w=w_1\cdots w_{d-1}\in\{L,R\}^{d-1}\) whose \(j\)-th letter is \(R\)
+exactly when \(j=e_1+\cdots+e_i\) for some \(i<r\), and is \(L\) otherwise.
+With
+\[
+ L=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\1&1\end{pmatrix},\qquad
+ R=\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\0&1\end{pmatrix},\qquad M_w=w_1\cdots w_{d-1},
+\]
+the map \((a_1,\ldots,a_r)\mapsto w\) is a bijection, and
+\begin{equation}
+ \begin{aligned}
+ \frac pq&=[0;a_1,\ldots,a_r]_-=
+ \frac{\text{sum of the first row of }M_w}{\|M_w\|_\Sigma},\\
+ q&=\mathcal D(a_1,\ldots,a_r)=\|M_w\|_\Sigma.
+ \end{aligned}
+ \label{eq:negative-cf-word-bijection}
+\end{equation}
+Thus \(d(p/q)=d\) is Knauf generation \(d\), the corresponding matrix word
+has length \(d-1\), and the generator cost is \(c(p/q)=2d+1\).  In particular,
+for \(\beta\in\mathbb R\), the even Knauf sum in the convention of
+\cite[Sec.~IV]{FialaKlebanOzluk2003} is exactly
+\[
+ Z^{K,e}_d(2\beta)
+ =\sum_{d(p/q)=d}q^{-2\beta}.
+\]
+There is no \(d=0\) fraction in \((0,1)\); the first four nonempty layers are
+\[
+\begin{array}{c|c|c|c}
+d&\text{words}&q=\|M_w\|_\Sigma\text{ in word order}&c=2d+1\\ \hline
+1&\varnothing&2&3\\
+2&L,R&3,\,3&5\\
+3&LL,LR,RL,RR&4,\,5,\,5,\,4&7\\
+4&\{L,R\}^3&5,\,7,\,8,\,7,\,7,\,8,\,7,\,5&9
+\end{array}
+\]
+\end{lemma}
+
+\begin{proof}
+Choosing the separator positions of a composition is the standard bijection
+between compositions of \(d\) and binary words of length \(d-1\).  Starting
+from \(M_\varnothing=I\), simultaneous induction on the word length, using
+right multiplication by \(L\) or \(R\), gives the displayed subtractive
+Euclidean recursion for the numerator and the negative-continuant recursion
+for the denominator.  Explicitly, appending \(L\) replaces the last
+composition part \(e_r\) by \(e_r+1\), whereas appending \(R\) replaces it by
+\((e_r,1)\); substituting these two operations in
+\(\mathcal D(a_1,\ldots,a_r)
+=a_r\mathcal D(a_1,\ldots,a_{r-1})
+-\mathcal D(a_1,\ldots,a_{r-2})\) agrees with
+\(M_{wL}=M_wL\) and \(M_{wR}=M_wR\), respectively.  This proves
+\eqref{eq:negative-cf-word-bijection}.
+The depth and cost identities follow from the definitions.  The Knauf
+identity is then term-by-term: its inverse temperature is \(2\beta\), so its
+denominator weight is \(q^{-2\beta}\).  The table also fixes the empty-word
+and first-generation conventions explicitly.
+\end{proof}

# Golden-ratio Bernoulli-convolution priority audit

Checked 2026-08-15 against primary papers or journal metadata.  This audit
uses the convention
\[
\tau_\mu(q)=\lim_{n\to\infty}
 \frac{\log\sum_{I\in\mathcal N_n}\mu(I)^q}{\log(\varphi^{-n})}.
\]

## Direct finite-count identification

- N. Sidorov and A. Vershik, *Ergodic properties of the Erdos measure, the
  entropy of the goldenshift, and related problems*, Monatsh. Math. 126
  (1998), 215-261, DOI 10.1007/BF01367764; preprint arXiv:math/9612223.
  Section 2 identifies \(f_n(k)\) both as the number of finite Fibonacci
  representations and as the vertex frequency on level \(n\) of the Fibonacci
  graph, and states that \(2^{-n}f_n(k)\) gives the finite masses converging to
  the Erdos measure.  Thus these are the manuscript's representation
  coefficients up to its unit/index convention, not an analogous object.

## Local dimensions and positive-q spectrum

- T.-Y. Hu, *The local dimensions of the Bernoulli convolution associated
  with the golden number*, Trans. Amer. Math. Soc. 349 (1997), 2917-2940,
  DOI 10.1090/S0002-9947-97-01474-8.  This predates the manuscript's
  normalized log-multiplicity discussion and determines extremal local
  dimensions for the same golden-ratio convolution.
- K.-S. Lau and S.-M. Ngai, *L^q-spectrum of the Bernoulli convolution
  associated with the golden ratio*, Studia Math. 131 (1998), 225-251.
  The paper's main result gives an exact formula for the golden-ratio
  \(L^q\)-spectrum for every \(q>0\).  Its block denominator
  \(Q_J=[1\ 1]P_J(1,1)^T\), block length \(2k+3\), and normalized mass
  \(2^{-(2k+3)}Q_J\) match, respectively, the renewal-letter denominator,
  letter cost, and normalized Fibonacci representation mass used here.

## All-real spectrum and negative phase transition

- D.-J. Feng and E. Olivier, *Multifractal analysis of weak Gibbs measures
  and phase transition: application to some Bernoulli convolutions*, Ergodic
  Theory Dynam. Systems 23 (2003), 1751-1784,
  DOI 10.1017/S0143385703000051.  Theorem 2.9 proves that the Erdos measure is
  weak Gibbs.  Theorem 2.10 gives its complete \(L^q\)-spectrum and a critical
  \(q_c<0\) such that the spectrum is linear exactly for \(q\le q_c\).
  Appendix Theorem A.1 proves nondifferentiability at the critical value.
  The paper explicitly calls this a first-order phase transition.
- D.-J. Feng, *The limited Rademacher functions and Bernoulli convolutions
  associated with Pisot numbers*, Adv. Math. 195 (2005), 24-101,
  DOI 10.1016/j.aim.2004.06.011.  Theorem 1.3(i), proved through the
  golden-ratio calculation in Theorem 4.4, gives the \(L^q\)-spectrum for
  every real \(q\).  It defines \(x(q)\) by the same matrix-block renewal
  series, proves a unique \(q_0<-2\), gives the positive-root branch for
  \(q>q_0\), sets \(x(q)=1\) for \(q\le q_0\), and proves that \(x\) is not
  differentiable at \(q_0\).  Feng's author bibliography records 1999
  preprints containing the underlying Parts I-II; the citable final article
  is 2005.

## Exact normalization and coverage

At level \(m\), a net-interval mass is \(2^{-m}f_m(k)\), while the geometric
scale is \(\varphi^{-m}\).  Therefore
\[
\log\sum_I\mu(I)^t=-mt\log2+\log\sum_k f_m(k)^t
\]
and hence, after the manuscript's finite layer/window index changes,
\[
P(t)=t\log2-\tau_\mu(t)\log\varphi.
\]
Consequently the prior literature covers: existence for every real tilt; the
positive implicit pressure equation; the negative linear/frozen phase; the
negative critical value as the solution of the block-renewal equation; its
first-order nondifferentiability; and the generic Legendre multifractal
formalism.  These cannot be novelty claims of the manuscript.

The present renewal alphabet sums over all reduced \(p/q\in(0,1)\).  At the
critical boundary its letter sum is
\[
\sum_{q\ge2}\varphi_{\rm E}(q)q^{-s}
=\frac{\zeta(s-1)}{\zeta(s)}-1.
\]
Thus Feng's implicit condition becomes
\(\zeta(\sigma_0-1)/\zeta(\sigma_0)=2\).  The located papers do not state this
totient/zeta evaluation, the critical constants \(2/\mu_C\) and \(4/\mu_C\),
the uniform finite-layer coexistence limit, the explicit orbit-filling lower
bound at nonexposed uniform-layer slopes, or the finite-window affine/fiber
identities.  Those are the defensible incremental claims, subject to the
proofs in the manuscript; absence from these sources is not a claim that no
other paper contains them.

The Editors
Transactions of the American Mathematical Society

Dear Editors,

We submit for your consideration the manuscript "Fibonacci Partition
Thermodynamics and Finite-Window Zeckendorf Fibers."

The paper studies the classical Fibonacci partition function R(n) on one
standard Fibonacci layer. Its principal result determines the moment pressure
for every real tilt. The pressure freezes at the exact threshold determined
by zeta(sigma_0-1)/zeta(sigma_0)=2, is analytic and strictly convex above the
threshold, and has a first-order corner there. At criticality the partition
sum has an explicit linear asymptotic and the Gibbs law has a uniform
coexistence limit. Under the uniform one-layer law, m^{-1} log R(n) satisfies
an unconditional full large deviation principle, including the nonexposed
slopes at the corner.

A finite-window Zeckendorf fold provides an exact finite model: its complete
fiber list is the list of R on two consecutive layers. An affine permutation
also identifies individual fibers with finite Fibonacci subset-sum
coefficients and partition-function differences. After a precise convention
translation, the known extremal classification gives every largest fiber and
maximizing residue. The frozen phase further rules out a globally analytic
compact-operator representation under stated spectral hypotheses.

The proof separates its published inputs, namely Weinstein's free-generator
decomposition and Stern--Brocot denominator pressure, from the new weighted
renewal, critical finite-size, and parity-free orbit-filling arguments.

The manuscript does not assert a finite-state normalization transducer,
collision kernel, recurrence-polynomial certificate, Galois-group result, or
Chebotarev theorem. Those claims and their computational artifacts have been
withdrawn.

The manuscript is in amsart format and is self-contained apart from
the cited results that are transferred with explicit convention dictionaries.

Thank you for considering this submission.

Sincerely,
The Authors

  319: \noindent\textbf{Erd\H{o}s measure and Bernoulli convolutions.}
  320: Sidorov--Vershik identify normalized finite Fibonacci representation
  321: frequencies with approximants to the Erd\H{o}s measure
  322: \cite{SidorovVershik1998}.  Hu studied its golden-ratio local dimensions
  323: \cite{Hu1997}; Lau--Ngai gave the exact \(L^q\)-spectrum for \(q>0\)
  324: \cite{LauNgai1998}; Feng--Olivier and Feng gave the complete real spectrum and
  325: the negative first-order transition \cite{FengOlivier2003,Feng2005}.  These
  326: are imported results, not merely related background.
  327: 
  328: If \(\mu\) denotes the golden-ratio Bernoulli convolution and
  329: \(\tau_\mu(t)\) uses the convention
  330: \(\tau_\mu(t)=\lim \log\sum_I\mu(I)^t/\log|I|\), then the level masses are
  331: \(2^{-m}\) times the finite Fibonacci representation counts.  Since the net
  332: scale is \(\varphi^{-m}\), the exact exponential-rate dictionary is
  333: \begin{equation}
  334:  P(t)=t\log2-\tau_\mu(t)\log\varphi.
  335:  \label{eq:erdos-pressure-dictionary}
  336: \end{equation}

  339: description are prior results.  The present paper adds the exact
  340: finite-window/fiber correspondences, evaluates Feng's implicit critical
  341: condition as \(\zeta(\sigma_0-1)/\zeta(\sigma_0)=2\), obtains the critical
  342: linear constants and uniform coexistence law, and proves the stated
  343: finite-layer nonexposed-slope lower bounds.
