\section{Finite-window fibers}\label{sec:preliminaries}

Throughout, the Fibonacci numbers are indexed by
\[
  F_0=0,
  \qquad
  F_1=F_2=1,
  \qquad
  F_{n+2}=F_{n+1}+F_n
  \qquad (n\ge 0).
\]

\subsection{Stable words and the residue model}

\begin{definition}[Stable words and visible values]
\label{def:stable-words}
For \(m\ge 0\), let
\[
  \Omega_m:=\{0,1\}^m,
  \qquad
  X_m:=\{x\in\Omega_m:\ x_ix_{i+1}=0\text{ for }1\le i<m\}.
\]
For \(x\in X_m\), define
\[
  V_m(x):=\sum_{j=1}^m x_jF_{j+1}.
\]
For \(\omega=\omega_1\cdots \omega_m\in\Omega_m\), define
\[
  N_m(\omega):=\sum_{j=1}^m \omega_jF_{j+1}.
\]
Thus \(\Omega_0=X_0=\{\varepsilon\}\), where \(\varepsilon\) is the empty word,
and \(V_0(\varepsilon)=N_0(\varepsilon)=0\).
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Visible values are complete]
\label{prop:Vm-bijection}
For every \(m\ge 0\), the map
\[
  V_m\colon X_m\longrightarrow \{0,1,\dots,F_{m+2}-1\}
\]
is a bijection.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The zero word maps to \(0\), so it remains to treat
\(n\in\{1,\dots,F_{m+2}-1\}\).  By Zeckendorf's theorem
\cite{Zeckendorf1972}, \(n\) has a unique
expansion as a sum of nonconsecutive Fibonacci numbers.  Because
\(n<F_{m+2}\), that expansion cannot use the summand \(F_{m+2}\) or any larger
Fibonacci number; therefore it uses only the weights \(F_2,\dots,F_{m+1}\),
and hence determines a unique word \(x\in X_m\) with \(V_m(x)=n\).
Conversely every \(x\in X_m\) is a stable expansion in those same weights, so
\(V_m(x)\in\{0,\dots,F_{m+2}-1\}\).  Thus \(V_m\) is bijective.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Finite-window fold]
\label{def:fold-map}
For \(m\ge 0\), define
\[
  \Fold_m(\omega):=
  V_m^{-1}\bigl(N_m(\omega)\bmod F_{m+2}\bigr),
  \qquad \omega\in\Omega_m.
\]
For \(x\in X_m\), define the fiber multiplicity
\[
  d_m(x):=\card{\Fold_m^{-1}(x)}.
\]
In particular, \(\Fold_0(\varepsilon)=\varepsilon\) and
\(d_0(\varepsilon)=1\).
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Single overflow]
\label{prop:single-overflow}
For every \(m\ge0\) and every \(\omega\in\Omega_m\),
\[
  N_m(\omega)\le \sum_{j=1}^m F_{j+1}=F_{m+3}-2<2F_{m+2}.
\]
Hence there exist unique integers \(b\in\{0,1\}\) and \(r\in[0,F_{m+2})\) such
that
\[
  N_m(\omega)=bF_{m+2}+r,
\]
and
\[
  \Fold_m(\omega)=V_m^{-1}(r).
\]
Equivalently, there is a unique overflow bit \(b_m(\omega)\in\{0,1\}\) such
that
\[
  N_m(\omega)=V_m(\Fold_m(\omega))+b_m(\omega)F_{m+2}.
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The Fibonacci identity
\(\sum_{j=1}^m F_{j+1}=F_{m+3}-2\) is standard, and
\(F_{m+3}-2<F_{m+3}-1<F_{m+3}=F_{m+2}+F_{m+1}<2F_{m+2}\).  The displayed
formula for \(\Fold_m\) is simply the definition.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Operational meaning of the fold]
\label{rem:prefix-normalization}
Because only one overflow can occur, the finite-window fold agrees with
computing the full Zeckendorf expansion of \(N_m(\omega)\) and deleting the
possible leading \(F_{m+2}\)-digit.
The residue definition is the useful one for exact fiber analysis.
\end{remark}

\begin{corollary}[Residue characterization of fibers]
\label{cor:residue-characterization}
For \(x\in X_m\), write \(r_x:=V_m(x)\).  Then
\[
  \Fold_m(\omega)=x
  \iff
  N_m(\omega)\equiv r_x \pmod{F_{m+2}}.
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
By definition,
\(\Fold_m(\omega)=V_m^{-1}(N_m(\omega)\bmod F_{m+2})\), and \(V_m\) is
bijective by \Cref{prop:Vm-bijection}.
\end{proof}

\begin{table}[ht]
\centering
\caption{Notation used throughout the paper.  The letter \(q\) is reserved
for a nonnegative integer moment order, whereas \(t\) denotes a real tilt.}
\label{tab:notation}
\begin{tabular}{@{}ll@{}}
\toprule
Symbol & Meaning \\
\midrule
\(\Omega_m\), \(X_m\) & raw binary words and legal visible words \\
\(\Fold_m\) & residue-defined finite-window fold \\
\(d_m(x)\), \(d_m^\#(r)\) & fiber multiplicity, indexed by a word or residue \\
\(\widetilde d_m(n)\) & coefficient of \(\prod_{j=1}^m(1+z^{F_j})\) \\
\(R(n)\), \(R^\dagger(n)\) & ordinary and indexed Fibonacci partition counts \\
\(S_q(m)\), \(S_t(m)\) & integer-order moment and real-tilt sum \\
\(T_q^\dagger(N)\) & indexed partition power sum below \(N\) \\
\(D_m\) & largest fiber multiplicity \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

    1: \section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}

    3: Let \(F_0=0\), \(F_1=F_2=1\), and
    4: \(F_{j+2}=F_{j+1}+F_j\), and put
    5: \(\varphi=(1+\sqrt5)/2\).  Let \(R(n)\) be the classical Fibonacci
    6: partition function: the number of partitions of \(n\) into distinct
    7: Fibonacci numbers, with the two unit terms identified.  Our main
    8: thermodynamic results concern \(R\) on the standard layers
    9: \[
   10:  \mathcal I_m=[F_{m+1}-1,F_{m+2}-1).
   11: \]
   12: The moment pressure for every real tilt and its first-order transition are
   13: already contained, under an affine normalization, in the golden-ratio
   14: Bernoulli-convolution literature \cite{LauNgai1998,FengOlivier2003,Feng2005}.
   15: We make that normalization explicit and use it as the starting point for
   16: finite-window identities, arithmetic critical refinements, and full
   17: large-deviation lower bounds for \(m^{-1}\log R(n)\) under the uniform law on
   18: \(\mathcal I_m\).
   19: 
   20: A finite-window Zeckendorf fold gives an exact finite model for this standard
   21: object.  A word
   22: \(\omega\in\{0,1\}^m\) determines the raw sum
   23: \[
   24:   N_m(\omega)=\sum_{j=1}^m\omega_jF_{j+1}.
   25: \]
   26: We consider the finite-window fold obtained by reducing this sum modulo
   27: \(F_{m+2}\) and taking the unique legal Zeckendorf word in the visible
   28: window.  If \(d_m(x)\) is the cardinality of the fiber over a legal word
   29: \(x\), the corresponding finite problem is to identify the multiset
   30: \(\{d_m(x):x\in X_m\}\) and its extremal and moment statistics.
   31: 
   32: The bridge is exact: the complete fiber list is the list of \(R\) on
   33: \(\mathcal I_m\sqcup\mathcal I_{m+1}\).  A second correspondence is an affine
   34: reindexing of a finite subset-sum spectrum.  Both are finite identities and
   35: do not require a normalization transducer.
   36: 
   37: \subsection{Main results}
   38: 
   39: Put \(R(n)=0\) for \(n<0\).  Let \(\sigma_0>2\) be the unique solution of
   40: \[
   41:  \frac{\zeta(\sigma_0-1)}{\zeta(\sigma_0)}=2;
   42:  \qquad \sigma_0=2.4787507857\ldots.
   43: \]
   44: For \(t>-\sigma_0\), let \(P(t)>0\) be the unique solution of
   45: \[
   46:  \sum_{q\ge2}\sum_{\substack{1\le p<q\\(p,q)=1}}
   47:  q^t e^{-P(t)c(p/q)}=1,
   48: \]
   49: where \(c(p/q)\) is the odd continued-fraction cost defined before
   50: \Cref{prop:weighted-generator-renewal}, and put \(P(t)=0\) for
   51: \(t\le-\sigma_0\).  Set
   52: \[
   53:  \begin{gathered}
   54:  \mu_C:=\sum_{q\ge2}\sum_{\substack{1\le p<q\\(p,q)=1}}
   55:  c(p/q)q^{-\sigma_0},\\
   56:  v_c:=\frac{\displaystyle
   57:  \sum_{q\ge2}\sum_{\substack{1\le p<q\\(p,q)=1}}
   58:  q^{-\sigma_0}\log q}{\mu_C},
   59:  \qquad
   60:  t_c:=-\sigma_0,\qquad
   61:  \alpha_+:=\frac12\log\varphi.
   62:  \end{gathered}
   63: \]
   64: Define
   65: \[
   66:  Z_m^R(t):=\sum_{n\in\mathcal I_m}R(n)^t.
   67: \]
   68: 
   69: \begin{theoremA}[One-layer Fibonacci partition thermodynamics]
   70: For every \(t\in\RR\),
   71: \[
   72:  \lim_{m\to\infty}\frac1m\log Z_m^R(t)=P(t).
   73: \]
   74: For \(s>\sigma_0\),
   75: \[
   76:  Z_m^R(-s)\longrightarrow
   77:  \frac2{2-\zeta(s-1)/\zeta(s)}-1,
   78: \]
   79: whereas \(Z_m^R(-\sigma_0)\sim2m/\mu_C\).  Under the critical Gibbs law
   80: proportional to \(R(n)^{-\sigma_0}\), the normalized log multiplicity and
   81: generating-layer cost converge jointly to \((v_cU,U)\), with \(U\) uniform on
   82: \([0,1]\).
   83: 
   84: If \(\mathbf N_m\) is uniform on \(\mathcal I_m\), then
   85: \(m^{-1}\log R(\mathbf N_m)\) satisfies a full large deviation principle
   86: with good rate function
   87: \[
   88:  I(\alpha)=\sup_{t\in\RR}\{t\alpha-P(t)+\log\varphi\}.
   89: \]
   90: Its effective domain is \([0,\alpha_+]\), and
   91: \(I(\alpha)=\log\varphi-\sigma_0\alpha\) on \([0,v_c]\).
   92: \end{theoremA}
   93: 
   94: This is \Cref{thm:standard-fibonacci-thermodynamics}.  Its proof uses the
   95: one-layer orbit weights \(2,1\), rather than deducing a statement about one
   96: component from the two-layer fiber mixture.
   97: 
   98: The same renewal representation retains the normalized generating cost
   99: \(B_m^R=m^{-1}\ell_m^R(\mathbf N_m)\).
  100: \Cref{thm:joint-cost-reward-ldp} proves a full LDP for
  101: \((B_m^R,Y_m^R)\).  Its rate is
  102: \[
  103:  J(\beta,\alpha)=
  104:  (1-\beta)\log\varphi+\beta I(\alpha/\beta)
  105:  \quad(0<\beta\le1,\ 0\le\alpha\le\beta\alpha_+),
  106: \]
  107: with \(J(0,0)=\log\varphi\) and \(J=+\infty\) elsewhere.  In particular,
  108: \[
  109:  J(\beta,\alpha)=\log\varphi-\sigma_0\alpha
  110:  \qquad(0\le\alpha\le\beta v_c),
  111: \]
  112: so coexistence is an affine face of the joint rate surface rather than an
  113: artifact of one-dimensional projection.  Fixed-cost exponential tilting
  114: supplies the strictly convex part; an exact one- or two-letter low-reward
  115: suffix fills every sufficiently large remaining cost on the affine face.
  116: 
  117: For the exact finite-window model, put
  118: \(R^\dagger(n)=R(n)+R(n-1)\).  Let
  119: \[
  120:   \widetilde d_m(n)=[z^n]\prod_{j=1}^m(1+z^{F_j}),
  121:   \qquad 0\le n<F_{m+2}.
  122: \]
  123: For residues modulo \(F_{m+2}\), define
  124: \[
  125:   \pi_m(n)\equiv F_{m+1}n+F_{m+1}-1\pmod{F_{m+2}}.
  126: \]
  127: 
  128: \begin{theoremB}[Affine fiber--partition correspondence]
  129: For every \(m\ge1\), the map \(\pi_m\) is a permutation and
  130: \[
  131:   d_m^\#(\pi_m(n))
  132:   =\widetilde d_m(n)
  133:   =R^\dagger(n)-R^\dagger(n-F_{m+1})
  134:   \qquad(0\le n<F_{m+2}).
  135: \]
  136: \end{theoremB}
  137: 
  138: This combines \Cref{thm:affine-transfer,thm:partition-difference}.  It gives a
  139: pointwise correspondence, not only an equality of aggregate moments.
  140: 
  141: There is also a direct interval description:
  142: \[
  143:  d_m^\#(r)=
  144:  \begin{cases}
  145:  R(F_{m+2}+r),&0\le r\le F_{m+1}-2,\\
  146:  R(r),&F_{m+1}-1\le r<F_{m+2}.
  147:  \end{cases}
  148: \]
  149: The known extremal classification of Koc\'abov\'a, Mas\'akov\'a, and
  150: Pelantov\'a can therefore be transferred exactly after an explicit change of
  151: Fibonacci convention.
  152: 
  153: \begin{theoremC}[Interval identity and largest fibers]
  154: Let \(D_m=\max_{x\in X_m}d_m(x)\).  Then
  155: \[
  156:  D_0=D_1=1,\qquad D_{2k}=F_{k+2}\ (k\ge0),\qquad
  157:  D_{2k+1}=2F_{k+1}\ (k\ge1),
  158: \]
  159: and \(D_m=D_{m-2}+D_{m-4}\) for every \(m\ge6\).  The maximizing residues,
  160: including the exceptional initial cases, are given in
  161: \Cref{thm:unconditional-extremal-fibers}.
  162: \end{theoremC}
  163: 
  164: For a real tilt \(t\), put
  165: \[
  166:   S_t(m)=\sum_{x\in X_m}d_m(x)^t.
  167: \]
  168: The level sets of \(R\) are sufficiently sparse to produce a frozen
  169: half-line.
  170: 
  171: \begin{theoremD}[Negative-temperature freezing]
  172: For every real \(t\le-\sigma_0\),
  173: \[
  174:   \lim_{m\to\infty}\frac1m\log S_t(m)=0.
  175: \]
  176: Consequently these pressure limits cannot be represented on all real tilts by
  177: the logarithm of a positive simple isolated eigenvalue of an analytic compact
  178: operator family on a fixed Banach space.
  179: \end{theoremD}

  470: \(j\ge2k\), the \(N_1\)-branches contribute one point in layer \(j\), and
  471: their \(\sigma\)-images contribute one point because the corresponding
  472: \(N_1\)-branches meet layer \(j-1\).  The two sheets are disjoint, so each
  473: generating orbit contributes exactly two points.  Hence
  474: \(N_j(k)=2\Psi(k)\) for \(j\ge2k\).  Weinstein's Theorem~5.1 supplies the
  475: same conclusion away from the endpoint; the preceding argument proves the
  476: additional case \(j=2k\).
  477: 
  478: Finally, Weinstein often writes the auxiliary layer with both endpoints.
  479: The shared endpoint has partition value \(1\), so this distinction is
  480: irrelevant for \(k\ge2\); the level \(1\) endpoint was assigned directly
  481: above.  This completes the treatment of endpoints and early layers.
  482: \end{proof}
  483: 
  484: We now show that an all-real analyticity ansatz cannot hold.  The obstruction
  485: is a proved frozen half-line for the Fibonacci partition spectrum.  Let
  486: \(\sigma_0>2\) be the unique real solution of
  487: \begin{equation}
  488:  \frac{\zeta(\sigma_0-1)}{\zeta(\sigma_0)}=2.
  489:  \label{eq:freezing-exponent}
  490: \end{equation}
  491: Numerically, \(\sigma_0=2.478750785733960\ldots\).
  492: The number \(-\sigma_0\) is initially obtained as a Dirichlet-series freezing
  493: bound.  The weighted renewal argument in
  494: \Cref{thm:exact-negative-pressure} subsequently proves that it is the exact
  495: zero-pressure boundary.
  496: 
  497: \begin{theorem}[Negative-temperature freezing]
  498: \label{thm:negative-temperature-freezing}
  499: For every real \(t\le-\sigma_0\), the pressure exists unconditionally and
  500: \[
  501:  P(t):=\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac1m\log S_t(m)=0.
  502: \]
  503: Consequently there is no real-analytic function on \(\RR\) which agrees with
  504: the finite-window pressure limits wherever those limits exist and also
  505: satisfies \(P(0)=\log\varphi\).  In particular, no everywhere finite pressure
  506: extension can be both real analytic and strictly convex on \(\RR\).
  507: \end{theorem}
  508: 
  509: \begin{proof}
  510: For \(s>2\), put
  511: \[
  512:  A(s):=\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}\frac{\phi_{\rm E}(n)}{n^s}
  513:  =\frac{\zeta(s-1)}{\zeta(s)}-1,
  514: \]
  515: where \(\phi_{\rm E}\) is Euler's totient function.  Weinstein's free-monoid
  516: decomposition and Tonelli's theorem give the following equality in
  517: \([0,+\infty]\):
  518: \begin{equation}
  519:  1+\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{\Psi(k)}{k^s}
  520:  =\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}A(s)^r.
  521:  \label{eq:weinstein-level-dirichlet-series}
  522: \end{equation}
  523: The geometric series is finite exactly when \(A(s)<1\), equivalently when
  524: \(\zeta(s-1)/\zeta(s)<2\); in that region its value is
  525: \((2-\zeta(s-1)/\zeta(s))^{-1}\).  The totient Dirichlet series also shows
  526: that the ratio is strictly decreasing from \(+\infty\) to \(1\) on
  527: \((2,\infty)\).  Hence \Cref{eq:freezing-exponent} has exactly one solution,
  528: and \Cref{eq:weinstein-level-dirichlet-series} is finite precisely for
  529: \(s>\sigma_0\).
  530: 
  531: By \Cref{thm:fiber-partition-interval}, the collision sum is exactly the sum
  532: of \(R(n)^t\) over two adjacent Fibonacci layers:
  533: \begin{equation}
  534:  S_t(m)=\sum_{n=F_{m+1}-1}^{F_{m+3}-2}R(n)^t.
  535:  \label{eq:real-collision-two-layers}
  536: \end{equation}
  537: If \(t<-\sigma_0\), the uniform layer-count lemma and
  538: \Cref{eq:weinstein-level-dirichlet-series} give, uniformly in \(m\),
  539: \[
  540:  1\le S_t(m)
  541:  \le 2+4\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\Psi(k)k^t<\infty.
  542: \]
  543: Thus \(m^{-1}\log S_t(m)\to0\).
  544: 
  545: At \(t=-\sigma_0\), fix \(\varepsilon>0\).  Only levels \(k\le D_m\)
  546: occur, and for each such level
  547: \[
  548:  k^{-\sigma_0}\le D_m^\varepsilon k^{-(\sigma_0+\varepsilon)}.
  549: \]
  550: The same layer-count estimate therefore gives
  551: \[
  552:  S_{-\sigma_0}(m)
  553:  \le 2+4D_m^{\varepsilon}
  554:        \sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{\Psi(k)}{k^{\sigma_0+\varepsilon}}.
  555: \]
  556: The series is finite, while
  557: \(m^{-1}\log D_m\to\frac12\log\varphi\) by
  558: \Cref{thm:unconditional-extremal-fibers}.  Therefore
  559: \[
  560:  0\le\limsup_{m\to\infty}\frac1m\log S_{-\sigma_0}(m)
  561:  \le\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\log\varphi.
  562: \]
  563: Letting \(\varepsilon\downarrow0\) proves the endpoint assertion.
  564: 
  565: Finally, an analytic function which vanishes on the open interval
  566: \(( -\infty,-\sigma_0)\) vanishes identically, whereas
  567: \[
  568:  P(0)=\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac1m\log|X_m|=\log\varphi.
  569: \]
  570: This proves the analytic and strict-convexity obstructions.
  571: \end{proof}
  572: 
  573: \begin{proposition}[No globally analytic compact-operator representation]
  574: \label{prop:no-analytic-compact-representation}
  575: There do not exist a complex Banach space \(\mathcal B\), a connected open
  576: set \(U\subset\mathbb C\) containing \(\RR\), and an operator-norm analytic
  577: map
  578: \[
  579:  z\longmapsto\mathcal L_z\in\mathcal K(\mathcal B)
  580:  \qquad(z\in U)
  581: \]
  582: into the compact operators with the following properties.  For every real
  583: \(t\), the operator \(\mathcal L_t\) has a positive algebraically simple
  584: isolated eigenvalue \(r(t)\); for every \(t_0\in\RR\), this eigenvalue is
  585: separated from the remainder of the spectrum by a positive gap uniformly for
  586: \(t\) in some real neighborhood of \(t_0\); and the family represents the
  587: finite-window sums in the exponential sense
  588: \[
  589:  \lim_{m\to\infty}\frac1m\log S_t(m)=\log r(t)
  590:  \qquad(t\in\RR).
  591: \]
  592: \end{proposition}
  593: 
  594: \begin{proof}
  595: For each \(m\ge1\), the function
  596: \[
  597:  p_m(t):=\frac1m\log S_t(m)
  598: \]
  599: is convex on \(\RR\).  Indeed, all fiber multiplicities are positive by the
  600: interval-completeness lemma, and H\"older's inequality gives the defining
  601: convexity inequality for \(S_t(m)\).  The representation hypothesis says that
  602: \(p_m(t)\) converges pointwise on \(\RR\) to
  603: \[
  604:  P(t):=\log r(t).
  605: \]
  606: The finite pointwise limit \(P\) is therefore convex.  A finite convex function
  607: on the open interval \(\RR\) is continuous, so \(r(t)=e^{P(t)}\) is continuous
  608: on \(\RR\).
  609: 
  610: Fix \(t_0\in\RR\).  Choose a positively oriented contour \(\Gamma\) enclosing
  611: \(r(t_0)\) and no other point of the spectrum of \(\mathcal L_{t_0}\).  For
  612: complex \(z\) sufficiently close to \(t_0\), the contour remains in the
  613: resolvent set and the Riesz projection
  614: \[
  615:  \Pi(z)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_\Gamma
  616:   (\lambda I-\mathcal L_z)^{-1}\,d\lambda
  617: \]
  618: is analytic.  Its rank is locally constant and equals the algebraic
  619: multiplicity of \(r(t_0)\), hence is one.  Analytic perturbation theory
  620: \cite[Chap.~VII, Sec.~1]{Kato1995} therefore supplies a unique analytic
  621: eigenvalue branch \(\mu(z)\) inside \(\Gamma\), with
  622: \(\mu(t_0)=r(t_0)\).  The continuity of \(r(t)\) puts \(r(t)\) inside
  623: \(\Gamma\) for all real \(t\) sufficiently close to \(t_0\); the local
  624: spectral separation and rank-one Riesz projection then force
  625: \(r(t)=\mu(t)\).  Thus \(r\), and hence \(t\mapsto\log r(t)\), is locally real
  626: analytic at every \(t_0\).
  627: 
  628: The representation hypothesis makes this function equal to \(P(t)\).  By
  629: \Cref{thm:negative-temperature-freezing}, it vanishes on
  630: \(( -\infty,-\sigma_0)\), and therefore vanishes identically.  This
  631: contradicts \(P(0)=\log\varphi\).
  632: \end{proof}
  633: 
  634: The all-real renewal analysis in \Cref{thm:all-real-pressure} shows that the
  635: pressure is real analytic and strictly convex on the positive-pressure phase,
  636: but it also shows that the present freezing transition has a genuine corner:
  637: the right derivative at \(-\sigma_0\) is strictly positive.  Thus the former
  638: differentiability hypothesis at the transition is false, and the
  639: G\"artner--Ellis theorem does not by itself provide lower bounds for slopes in
  640: the interval between the two one-sided derivatives.  The finite-window
  641: orbit-filling construction in \Cref{thm:full-fiber-ldp} provides precisely
  642: these missing lower bounds and yields an unconditional full large deviation
  643: principle.

# Fibonacci Partition Thermodynamics and Finite-Window Zeckendorf Fibers

This directory contains the refocused main manuscript
`Fibonacci Partition Thermodynamics and Finite-Window Zeckendorf Fibers` and
a separately compiled supplement.

## Scope

The compiled paper retains only the independently justified core:

- the exact dictionary from the previously known golden-ratio Bernoulli
  convolution \(L^q\)-spectrum to Fibonacci partition layers;
- the arithmetic critical point, critical Gibbs law, and full finite-layer
  LDP on a single standard Fibonacci partition layer;
- the joint generation-cost and log-multiplicity LDP, including its affine
  two-dimensional coexistence face;
- the residue and affine fiber correspondence;
- the pointwise Fibonacci partition-difference formula;
- the exact two-layer interval identity and transferred extremal theorem;
- the uniform Weinstein layer-count lemma and negative-temperature freezing;
- the linear critical finite-size law and uniform critical coexistence limit;
- the precise compact-operator obstruction;
- the full real-tilt large deviation principle across the freezing corner;
- the exact finite-prime-support coefficient interface and the explicit
  heavy-cost obstruction at the active cutoff.

The conditional microcanonical refinements, exact dyadic ray, integer moment
transfer, direct quadratic recurrence, and high-tilt consequences are in
`supplement.pdf`, not in the main theorem spine.

The invalid normalization transducer, collision automaton, histogram kernels,
arithmetic-window recurrence certificates, Galois computations, and
Chebotarev claims have been withdrawn. Their section sources and certificate
package are not part of this directory.

## Build

The bibliography is written directly in `sec_references.tex`; BibTeX is not
used. Compile the main article before the supplement so `xr-hyper` can resolve
cross-document references:

```powershell
latexmk -pdfxe -interaction=nonstopmode -halt-on-error main.tex
latexmk -pdfxe -interaction=nonstopmode -halt-on-error supplement.tex
```

The expected outputs are `main.pdf` and `supplement.pdf`.

The manuscript is currently 59 pages with 41 active theorem environments. A partial repair is already present in the dirty worktree: it added the five missing literature sources, began the priority rewrite, expanded the Stern–Brocot/Knauf normalization argument, and hid the finite-prime directional theorem behind `\iffalse`. It did not complete the JNT retargeting, physical deletion, structural compression, line-limit repair, or final verification.

Three viable approaches remain:

1. **Conservative JNT repair, recommended.** Retain the finite-window identities, exact critical arithmetic, finite-size constants, coexistence laws, and full LDP. Present the pressure and negative-\(q\) phase transition strictly as recoveries of Lau–Ngai, Feng–Olivier, and Feng. Keep and tighten the expanded normalization proof. Physically delete Theorem 5.12 and its dependent directional claims, retaining only the exact finite-prime-support generating function.

2. **Proof-heavy retention.** Keep Theorem 5.12 by supplying parameter-uniform multivariate smooth-point asymptotics and a complete unbounded-cost tail argument. This would require substantial new technical statements, conflict with “do not add new theorems,” lengthen the transfer-theoretic portion, and work against the JNT retargeting.

3. **Minimal arithmetic paper.** Remove most pressure and LDP material and publish only the finite-window identities, extremal transfers, zeta critical point, and critical constants. This minimizes proof risk but discards several surviving contributions the referee considered publishable.

For approach 1, the repair design is:

- Retitle and reframe the paper for Journal of Number Theory, with Fibonacci partitions and finite-window identities first and thermodynamic terminology secondary.
- Rewrite the abstract and main-result summaries so the known all-real pressure, implicit positive branch, frozen phase, phase transition, and generic Legendre formalism are never presented as new.
- State an exact finite-count dictionary: Sidorov–Vershik’s representation frequencies are the coefficients of the same finite Fibonacci subset-sum product, with the manuscript’s indexing shift made explicit; the folded fibers are the two possible coefficients separated by \(F_{m+2}\).
- Retain accurate citations to Sidorov–Vershik, Hu, Lau–Ngai, Feng–Olivier, and Feng, together with the exact normalization
  \[
  P(t)=t\log 2-\tau_\mu(t)\log\varphi.
  \]
